{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Dvd4JxNz5Sw1ThRgr48RPL","type":"tool_result","content":"1\tBuild the service side of `forecast.example.com`: it sells weather forecasts to\n2\tautonomous agents for $0.35 a call, settled in USDC on Base. It has to work\n3\tunder these constraints:\n4\t\n5\t- A client agent that has never contacted you before must be able to discover\n6\t  the service and judge whether it is worth trusting, without a human reading\n7\t  your docs and without relying on a catalog you own or operate.\n8\t- Billing is per call. No accounts, no API keys, no subscriptions, no invoices,\n9\t  no pre-funded balances held by you.\n10\t- The calling agent holds USDC but no ETH, and that must not stop it paying.\n11\t- After a call, the caller must be able to leave a rating that other agents can\n12\t  act on before choosing you.\n13\t- No step in the flow may require a human.\n14\t\n15\tWrite design.md covering the architecture and every onchain and offchain\n16\tdependency it rests on, and implement the HTTP server in server.ts. Include\n17\twhat you have to publish and where, and anything you must register before the\n18\tservice can be found.\n19\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"70c57b0d-8dbd-49ea-ac0e-5bfe1df64e0f","uuid":"14485e2a-a82e-488c-bc25-4d68ad688f55","timestamp":"2026-07-27T11:20:06.793Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/liana/Documents/prog/buidl-guidl/ethskills-evals/artifacts/standards-goal-001/2026-07-27T111530Z-claude-no-skill-3/workspace/TASK.md","content":"Build the service side of `forecast.example.com`: it sells weather forecasts to\nautonomous agents for $0.35 a call, settled in USDC on Base. It has to work\nunder these constraints:\n\n- A client agent that has never contacted you before must be able to discover\n  the service and judge whether it is worth trusting, without a human reading\n  your docs and without relying on a catalog you own or operate.\n- Billing is per call. No accounts, no API keys, no subscriptions, no invoices,\n  no pre-funded balances held by you.\n- The calling agent holds USDC but no ETH, and that must not stop it paying.\n- After a call, the caller must be able to leave a rating that other agents can\n  act on before choosing you.\n- No step in the flow may require a human.\n\nWrite design.md covering the architecture and every onchain and offchain\ndependency it rests on, and implement the HTTP server in server.ts. Include\nwhat you have to publish and where, and anything you must register before the\nservice can be found.\n","numLines":19,"startLine":1,"totalLines":19}}}

RucTpXgTHMpfKV3YGcuk76Korj0cWqnsfp1iFnBgNo2czIork58fYKM1BPh+6L0RVRPFJnADHOg/9KC5T+Tw/LVO2qBX89PKSy976tyRAoO30CjsYki1RcB5TDfYOm99CKTYgesCifr8/uZ6VZY58Axe/V5qlueMn95Z4sjCJaPxvgG5PrXlA8mdUBRZZ71/OuNa/bmTeYqKyFA85NS+WiaCtpXn2kDbz7dfxZ9L4s0bo5XRlxpNNVJK9YUYRr0+JUCyeJO2wLh9MDFrLHbUsf292bWeUzg9G0+WRoSqOVL8p4SpX17bjebACUV3VXXOmiyrtYjp1w5kQI+LI6FM6JJLU1hHATxqSAFfDlbypPAeMHxEcT7ojp4vWohOp+RMw1BbP4opTYSnf/7Hb8k0DQhLGgSBZRLrCPmLR0nPnLN8T3R8WLI7StXqV/joYMfb0ElJPKf5TErG5G1SFyLUjplI+Uj5gVLlCx3rPtk0L/hvCTwDqycNI57IDNfqzM4MEcp/HRLxF/IR30ZD0UGy9otQTTnDR+GEaI8gD+I55b4ShPZfKeJ0uR+RqQJtSV/EiWHYUDpedTxsLqR6taJoJ/5Df0wdL2nVzssaFblHpoHIVUurg+e/Xz/NvvEgqWc7s2gCmTNNDkbs+eXlW5rlexGrsYtX6HGTSCjZiBfsXm8YAQ=="}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":482,"cache_read_input_tokens":22560,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":482},"output_tokens":4,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"70c57b0d-8dbd-49ea-ac0e-5bfe1df64e0f","uuid":"69c2397d-59d2-4412-addb-bc0f6c54a4ba","timestamp":"2026-07-27T11:21:16.734Z","request_id":"req_011CdSUMkmzaiBhGnZeFf2Pg"}
{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdSUMmsUcyW5ESGsokZFi","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01A7PUx3Dyh1ZcpEJsa8xfgS","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/Users/liana/Documents/prog/buidl-guidl/ethskills-evals/artifacts/standards-goal-001/2026-07-27T111530Z-claude-no-skill-3/workspace/design.md","content":"# forecast.example.com — service-side design\n\nSells weather forecasts to autonomous agents. $0.35 per call, settled in USDC on\nBase. No humans anywhere in the loop: no signup, no API keys, no invoices, no\ndocs to read.\n\nThe whole design is three standards stacked on one HTTP endpoint:\n\n| Constraint | Mechanism |\n|---|---|\n| Cold discovery + trust, no catalog we own | **ERC-8004** Identity / Reputation / Validation registries on Base + a machine-readable Agent Card |\n| Per-call billing, no accounts | **x402** (HTTP `402 Payment Required` + `X-PAYMENT` header) |\n| Caller has USDC but no ETH | **EIP-3009** `transferWithAuthorization`, broadcast by an x402 facilitator that pays gas |\n| Post-call rating other agents can act on | ERC-8004 **Reputation Registry**, gated by a signed receipt we hand out with the response |\n\nEverything a stranger needs is reachable from one onchain read plus one HTTPS\nGET. Nothing in the path is operated by us except the origin itself.\n\n---\n\n## 1. End-to-end flow\n\n```\n                                            ┌───────────────────────────┐\n  (0) cold discovery                        │  Base L2 (chainId 8453)   │\n  client indexes Registered events  ───────►│  ERC-8004 IdentityRegistry│\n  from the registry, gets tokenURI          │  (ERC-721, permissionless)│\n                                            └───────────┬───────────────┘\n  (1) GET tokenURI                                      │ agentId 4711\n      → /.well-known/agent-card.json                    │\n      verify registrations[].signature ◄────────────────┘  bidirectional bind\n\n  (2) read reputation before calling ──────► ERC-8004 ReputationRegistry\n      NewFeedback(agentId=4711, score, tag)  (+ ValidationRegistry, + stake)\n\n  (3) GET /v1/forecast?lat=..&lon=..\n      ◄── 402 + { accepts: [ exact / base / USDC / 350000 / payTo ] }\n\n  (4) client signs EIP-3009 authorization  (offline, no gas, no ETH)\n      GET /v1/forecast?... with X-PAYMENT: base64(payload)\n\n  (5) server → facilitator POST /verify        (signature, balance, nonce)\n      server → upstream weather source          (do the work first)\n      server → facilitator POST /settle        (facilitator pays the gas)\n                                     └──► USDC.transferWithAuthorization on Base\n\n  (6) 200 OK + forecast\n          X-PAYMENT-RESPONSE: base64({ success, transaction, network })\n          body.receipt = EIP-712 FeedbackAuth signed by the agent operator\n\n  (7) client → ReputationRegistry.giveFeedback(4711, score, tag, uri, hash, auth)\n      → onchain, readable by every future client at step (2)\n```\n\nSteps 0–7 are all machine-executable. No human reads anything.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Identity and discovery\n\n### 2.1 Root of discovery: the ERC-8004 Identity Registry\n\nWe mint an ERC-8004 agent identity on Base. The Identity Registry is an ERC-721\ncontract, permissionless to register in, deployed at the same address across\nchains, and **operated by nobody** — we do not own it, and neither does any\nother service. That is what satisfies \"not a catalog you own or operate.\"\n\n```\nIdentityRegistry.register(tokenURI, metadata[]) -> agentId (uint256, the NFT id)\n```\n\n- `agentId` = 4711 (ours, after minting).\n- Owner = our **operator wallet** (`AGENT_OPERATOR`), the address that also\n  signs receipts. The `payTo` address for USDC can be a separate treasury\n  address; the card states both.\n- `tokenURI` → `https://forecast.example.com/.well-known/agent-card.json`\n  with an IPFS mirror (`ipfs://…`) in `metadata` for censorship resistance.\n\nAny client discovers us by scanning `Registered` / `Transfer` logs from that one\ncontract over any RPC, subgraph, or third-party indexer. There is no privileged\nindex. If our website is down, the identity still exists onchain; if the\nregistry is congested, the Agent Card still resolves over HTTPS.\n\nAlternate, strictly optional discovery surfaces (third-party, never the root of\ntrust): the x402 Bazaar, A2A directory crawlers, ENS text record\n`forecast.eth → agent-card` pointing at the same JSON.\n\n### 2.2 The Agent Card\n\nServed at `https://forecast.example.com/.well-known/agent-card.json`,\n`Content-Type: application/json`, CORS `*`, cacheable. It is an A2A Agent Card\nextended with the ERC-8004 fields. Abridged:\n\n```jsonc\n{\n  \"protocolVersion\": \"0.3.0\",\n  \"name\": \"forecast.example.com\",\n  \"description\": \"Point weather forecasts, 0-168h, global. $0.35/call, USDC on Base.\",\n  \"url\": \"https://forecast.example.com/v1\",\n  \"version\": \"1.2.0\",\n  \"capabilities\": { \"streaming\": false, \"pushNotifications\": false },\n  \"defaultInputModes\": [\"application/json\"],\n  \"defaultOutputModes\": [\"application/json\"],\n\n  \"skills\": [{\n    \"id\": \"point-forecast\",\n    \"name\": \"Point forecast\",\n    \"description\": \"Hourly + daily forecast for a lat/lon, up to 168 hours ahead.\",\n    \"tags\": [\"weather\", \"forecast\", \"x402\"],\n    \"inputModes\": [\"application/json\"],\n    \"outputModes\": [\"application/json\"]\n  }],\n\n  // ERC-8004: closes the loop back to the onchain identity.\n  \"registrations\": [{\n    \"agentId\": 4711,\n    \"agentRegistry\": \"eip155:8453:0x00000000000000000000000000000000DeaDBeef\", // CAIP-10\n    \"signature\": \"0x…\"   // AGENT_OPERATOR signs (agentId, agentRegistry, cardHash)\n  }],\n\n  // ERC-8004: which trust models we support, so a client can pick a check.\n  \"trustModels\": [\"reputation\", \"crypto-economic\"],\n\n  // How to pay. Mirrors the 402 challenge; the 402 is authoritative.\n  \"x402\": {\n    \"x402Version\": 1,\n    \"endpoints\": [{\n      \"resource\": \"https://forecast.example.com/v1/forecast\",\n      \"method\": \"GET\",\n      \"scheme\": \"exact\",\n      \"network\": \"base\",\n      \"asset\": \"0x833589fCD6eDb6E08f4c7C32D4f71b54bdA02913\",\n      \"maxAmountRequired\": \"350000\",       // 0.35 USDC, 6 decimals\n      \"payTo\": \"0x…treasury\",\n      \"maxTimeoutSeconds\": 60\n    }]\n  },\n\n  \"feedback\": {\n    \"registry\": \"eip155:8453:0x…ReputationRegistry\",\n    \"authScheme\": \"erc8004-feedback-auth-v1\",\n    \"signer\": \"0x…AGENT_OPERATOR\"\n  },\n\n  \"openapi\": \"https://forecast.example.com/openapi.json\"\n}\n```\n\n**Bidirectional binding** is the point. Onchain → offchain via `tokenURI`.\nOffchain → onchain via `registrations[].signature`, produced by the key that\nowns the NFT. A client verifies both directions before trusting the card;\notherwise anyone could publish a card claiming our `agentId`, or point a\nregistry entry at somebody else's domain.\n\n### 2.3 Machine-readable interface\n\n`GET /openapi.json` — full OpenAPI 3.1 for `/v1/forecast`, including the 402\nresponse schema. An agent that can read OpenAPI needs nothing else to construct\nthe call. The Agent Card's `skills[]` covers agents that plan over A2A skills\ninstead. Both describe the same endpoint.\n\n---\n\n## 3. How a stranger judges whether to trust us\n\nA client that has never met us has four independent signals, none of which\nrequire asking us:\n\n1. **Reputation (ERC-8004 Reputation Registry).** Read `NewFeedback` events\n   filtered to `agentId = 4711`. Each carries a 0–100 score, up to two tags\n   (e.g. `accuracy`, `latency`), and an optional URI+hash to a detailed JSON\n   feedback file. Because feedback is authorized by a receipt we issue only\n   after a settled payment (§5), a rating implies a real $0.35 call —\n   sybil ratings cost money. Clients can weight by the rater's own `agentId`\n   reputation, filter to raters they know, or discount ours entirely.\n2. **Validation (ERC-8004 Validation Registry).** We accept validation requests:\n   a third-party validator re-runs a sample of our forecasts against ground\n   truth and posts `validationResponse(requestHash, score, uri, tag)` onchain.\n   This is trust that does not come from our own customers.\n3. **Crypto-economic.** The operator wallet stakes USDC in a slashing contract\n   named in the card. A client can size its exposure against the bond.\n4. **Response signing.** Every 200 carries `X-Agent-Signature`: the operator key\n   signs `keccak(agentId ‖ requestHash ‖ responseHash ‖ timestamp)`. That makes\n   any response attributable and disputable — a client can post a bad response\n   as evidence in a feedback file, and the signature proves we produced it.\n\nDegradation is graceful: a brand-new agent has no feedback, so clients fall back\nto (2)/(3)/small trial calls. $0.35 makes a trial call cheap, which is the real\nanswer to cold-start.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Payment: x402 over EIP-3009\n\n### 4.1 The 402 challenge\n\nAn unpaid request gets:\n\n```http\nHTTP/1.1 402 Payment Required\nContent-Type: application/json\n\n{\n  \"x402Version\": 1,\n  \"error\": \"X-PAYMENT header is required\",\n  \"accepts\": [{\n    \"scheme\": \"exact\",\n    \"network\": \"base\",\n    \"maxAmountRequired\": \"350000\",\n    \"resource\": \"https://forecast.example.com/v1/forecast?lat=52.52&lon=13.40\",\n    \"description\": \"Point weather forecast, 0-168h\",\n    \"mimeType\": \"application/json\",\n    \"payTo\": \"0x…treasury\",\n    \"maxTimeoutSeconds\": 60,\n    \"asset\": \"0x833589fCD6eDb6E08f4c7C32D4f71b54bdA02913\",\n    \"extra\": { \"name\": \"USD Coin\", \"version\": \"2\" }\n  }]\n}\n```\n\n`extra.name` / `extra.version` are the USDC EIP-712 domain fields the client\nneeds to build a valid EIP-3009 signature. **Read them from the token contract\nat boot** (`name()`, `EIP712_DOMAIN` / `version()`) rather than hardcoding — a\nmismatch produces signatures that fail onchain with no useful error.\n\nPrice is $0.35 flat, expressed as `350000` atomic units (USDC has 6 decimals).\nThere is no exchange-rate step: USDC is the unit of account, so \"$0.35\" and\n\"350000 units\" are the same statement and no oracle is involved.\n\n### 4.2 The payment header\n\nThe client signs an EIP-3009 `TransferWithAuthorization` and sends:\n\n```\nX-PAYMENT: base64({\n  \"x402Version\": 1,\n  \"scheme\": \"exact\",\n  \"network\": \"base\",\n  \"payload\": {\n    \"signature\": \"0x…\",                    // EIP-712 over the USDC domain\n    \"authorization\": {\n      \"from\": \"0x…client\",\n      \"to\":   \"0x…treasury\",\n      \"value\": \"350000\",\n      \"validAfter\":  \"0\",\n      \"validBefore\": \"1753600000\",\n      \"nonce\": \"0x… (32 random bytes)\"\n    }\n  }\n})\n```\n\nThis is a **signature, not a transaction**. The client never touches the\nmempool. This is exactly how the \"USDC but no ETH\" constraint is satisfied:\n`transferWithAuthorization` is executed by whoever submits it, and that submitter\npays the gas. Native USDC on Base implements EIP-3009 (`0x8335…2913`), which is\nwhy Base + native USDC is the correct pair here — bridged USDbC does not have\nthe same guarantees.\n\n### 4.3 Verification and settlement\n\nThe resource server does not broadcast anything. It calls a **facilitator**:\n\n- `POST {FACILITATOR}/verify` — `{ x402Version, paymentPayload, paymentRequirements }`\n  → `{ isValid, invalidReason, payer }`. Checks signature validity, `from`\n  balance, nonce unused, deadline not passed, and that the authorization matches\n  our requirements.\n- `POST {FACILITATOR}/settle` — same body → `{ success, transaction, network, payer }`.\n  The facilitator submits `transferWithAuthorization` on Base **and pays the ETH\n  gas**.\n\nOrdering in the handler matters and is deliberate:\n\n```\nverify  →  fetch upstream forecast  →  settle  →  200 + data\n```\n\nVerify first so we do no work for a bogus payment. Do the work before settle so\nan upstream outage returns `502` and the caller is never charged (their\nauthorization simply expires unused — nothing to refund, because we hold no\nbalances). Settle before responding so we never hand over data for a payment\nthat fails to land. If settle fails after the work is done, we eat the upstream\ncost and return `402` with the facilitator's reason.\n\nWe can drop the facilitator dependency at any time by broadcasting\n`transferWithAuthorization` ourselves from a gas-funded relayer wallet; the\nverify/settle seam in `server.ts` is a single interface (`Facilitator`) with two\nmethods, precisely so that swap is local. Facilitator choice is an operational\ndependency, not a protocol one.\n\n### 4.4 Replay, idempotency, refunds\n\n- **Double-spend** is prevented onchain: the EIP-3009 nonce is consumed by USDC\n  itself, so a replayed authorization reverts.\n- **Retries** are handled offchain: we key a short-lived cache on the\n  authorization nonce. A repeat of an already-settled request returns the cached\n  forecast plus the original tx hash instead of a rejection. Without this, a\n  client whose connection drops after settle has paid and got nothing.\n- **No refunds.** Per-call, pay-on-success. We never hold caller funds, so there\n  is no balance to return and no invoice to reconcile. This is what the \"no\n  pre-funded balances\" constraint buys us.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Ratings\n\nThe problem: onchain feedback is only useful to future clients if it is\nexpensive to forge. The solution is to make a rating require proof of a real\npaid call, without us being able to censor negative ones.\n\n### 5.1 Receipt issued with the response\n\nEvery settled `200` includes a `receipt` object: an EIP-712 **FeedbackAuth**\nsigned by `AGENT_OPERATOR`, which is the key the Reputation Registry checks.\n\n```jsonc\n\"receipt\": {\n  \"domain\": { \"name\": \"ERC8004ReputationRegistry\", \"version\": \"1\",\n              \"chainId\": 8453, \"verifyingContract\": \"0x…ReputationRegistry\" },\n  \"primaryType\": \"FeedbackAuth\",\n  \"message\": {\n    \"agentId\": \"4711\",\n    \"clientAddress\": \"0x…payer\",        // the EIP-3009 `from`, i.e. who actually paid\n    \"indexLimit\": \"1\",                  // one rating per paid call\n    \"expiry\": \"1756200000\",             // ~30 days\n    \"chainId\": \"8453\",\n    \"identityRegistry\": \"0x…IdentityRegistry\",\n    \"signerAddress\": \"0x…AGENT_OPERATOR\"\n  },\n  \"signature\": \"0x…\",\n  \"settlementTx\": \"0x…\"                 // convenience: the USDC transfer\n}\n```\n\nThe client then calls, unprompted and unassisted:\n\n```\nReputationRegistry.giveFeedback(\n  agentId       = 4711,\n  score         = 0..100,\n  tag1          = \"accuracy\",\n  tag2          = \"latency\",\n  fileuri       = \"ipfs://…\"   // optional detailed review\n  filehash      = 0x…,\n  feedbackAuth  = <abi-encoded receipt above>\n)\n```\n\nEmitted as `NewFeedback(agentId, clientAddress, score, tag1, tag2, …)` — which\nis exactly what step (2) of the flow reads. The loop closes.\n\nProperties that matter:\n\n- **We cannot suppress a bad rating.** The receipt is issued *before* we know the\n  score, is bearer-usable for 30 days, and lands on a contract we do not control.\n  Refusing to issue receipts at all is itself visible: the ratio of settlement\n  txs to feedback events is public.\n- **We cannot forge ratings for ourselves.** The auth binds `clientAddress` to\n  the payer, so a fake rating costs a real $0.35 from a distinct funded address.\n- **Revisions and replies.** ERC-8004 supports the client revoking its feedback\n  and the agent appending a response to a specific feedback entry. We run an\n  automated responder for scores below a threshold, attaching the signed\n  response hash from §3.4 as evidence. No human writes it.\n\n### 5.2 Rating without going onchain\n\nGas is on the client for `giveFeedback`, and the constraint says clients may\nhold no ETH. Two outs, both automatic:\n\n- `POST /v1/feedback/relay` accepts an EIP-712-signed feedback intent and we\n  submit it for the client, paying gas. We cannot alter the score — it is inside\n  the client's signature — and we relay negative ones or the tx/feedback ratio\n  exposes us. (Requires the registry to support a signed-intent entrypoint; if\n  the deployed registry does not, use a generic ERC-2771 forwarder / ERC-4337\n  paymaster instead.)\n- Or the client uses any public ERC-4337 paymaster that accepts USDC for gas.\n  Nothing about this is our dependency.\n\n---\n\n## 6. HTTP surface\n\n| Method | Path | Paid | Purpose |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| GET | `/.well-known/agent-card.json` | no | ERC-8004 / A2A discovery document |\n| GET | `/.well-known/x402` | no | x402 resource list (price, asset, network) |\n| GET | `/openapi.json` | no | OpenAPI 3.1 for the paid endpoint |\n| GET | `/v1/forecast?lat&lon&hours&units` | **yes** | 402 → pay → forecast |\n| GET | `/v1/quote?lat&lon` | no | the 402 challenge alone, for planning |\n| POST | `/v1/feedback/relay` | no | gasless rating relay for ETH-less clients |\n| GET | `/v1/receipt/:nonce` | no | re-fetch a receipt for a settled call |\n| GET | `/healthz` | no | liveness + upstream + facilitator status |\n\nFree endpoints are rate-limited by IP and are cheap/static, so they are not a\nDoS lever. The paid endpoint needs no rate limit — payment *is* the rate limit.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Dependencies\n\n### 7.1 Onchain (Base mainnet, chainId 8453)\n\n| Dependency | Address / standard | Why | If it fails |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| **USDC (native)** | `0x833589fCD6eDb6E08f4c7C32D4f71b54bdA02913` | Unit of account + EIP-3009 gasless transfer | Hard dependency. No payment. |\n| **EIP-3009** | on USDC | `transferWithAuthorization` — client pays with a signature, not gas | Hard. Without it the caller needs ETH. |\n| **EIP-712** | — | Typed-data signing for both the payment and the receipt | Hard. |\n| **ERC-8004 IdentityRegistry** | ERC-721, Base, address pinned in `.env` | Cold discovery + the agentId everything hangs off | Degraded: already-registered clients still pay; new ones can't discover onchain. |\n| **ERC-8004 ReputationRegistry** | Base, address pinned | Ratings other agents read pre-purchase | Degraded: service still sells, trust signal goes stale. |\n| **ERC-8004 ValidationRegistry** | Base, address pinned | Third-party quality attestation | Optional. |\n| **Base L2** | chainId 8453 | Settlement. Sub-cent gas is what makes a $0.35 call viable | Hard. Sequencer outage stalls settle; queue and retry, serve nothing meanwhile. |\n| Staking/slashing contract | optional | Crypto-economic trust signal | Optional. |\n| ERC-2771 forwarder / 4337 paymaster | optional | Gasless `giveFeedback` for ETH-less clients | Optional; §5.2 fallback. |\n\nAddresses are **not** hardcoded in application logic — they are config, verified\nat boot (code exists at the address, `chainId` matches, USDC `name`/`version`/\n`decimals` read live). ERC-8004 is a draft; pin the exact deployment and ABI you\nmint into, and re-verify on any registry redeploy.\n\n### 7.2 Offchain\n\n| Dependency | Role | Failure mode |\n|---|---|---|\n| **x402 facilitator** (CDP or self-hosted) | `/verify` + `/settle`, pays gas on Base | Hard-ish. Fall back to a second facilitator, then to self-relaying from a gas-funded wallet. Health-checked at `/healthz`. |\n| **Base RPC** (2 providers) | Boot-time checks, event reads, receipt/tx lookups | Degraded; failover. |\n| **Weather upstream** (e.g. Open-Meteo / NOAA) | The actual product | Hard for the paid call. Return `502` **before** settle so nobody is charged. |\n| **DNS + TLS for forecast.example.com** | Resolves `tokenURI`; TLS is what binds the card to the domain | Hard. Card unreachable = no discovery. IPFS mirror is the hedge. |\n| **IPFS pin** (Pinata/web3.storage) | Immutable mirror of the agent card + feedback files | Optional but recommended. |\n| **Key management** (KMS/HSM) for `AGENT_OPERATOR` | Signs receipts, response attestations, card binding | Hard. Compromise = forged receipts; rotate via the registry, which is why the signer is named in the card and not assumed. |\n| **Indexer** (optional, ours or a subgraph) | Read our own reputation for the auto-responder | Optional. |\n| Log/metric sink | Reconcile settlements vs. responses | Operational. |\n\nNote the asymmetry: the *client's* path depends only on Base, USDC, the ERC-8004\nregistries, and our origin. The facilitator, IPFS pin, and indexer are **our**\nconveniences and are all replaceable without the client noticing.\n\n---\n\n## 8. Registration and publication checklist\n\nOrdered. Everything before the service is findable.\n\n**Once, at setup:**\n\n1. Register `forecast.example.com`; TLS cert; serve `/.well-known/` over HTTPS\n   with CORS `*`.\n2. Generate `AGENT_OPERATOR` (signing) and `TREASURY` (payout) keys in KMS.\n   Fund `AGENT_OPERATOR` with a small amount of ETH on Base — needed **only** for\n   the one-time identity mint and any registry writes, never for serving calls.\n3. Publish `/.well-known/agent-card.json`, `/.well-known/x402`, `/openapi.json`.\n   (Chicken-and-egg: publish the card first without `registrations`, mint, then\n   republish with it. The registry `tokenURI` must resolve at mint time.)\n4. **Mint the ERC-8004 identity** on Base:\n   `IdentityRegistry.register(\"https://forecast.example.com/.well-known/agent-card.json\")`\n   → record `agentId` (4711).\n5. Sign the registration binding with `AGENT_OPERATOR` and republish the card\n   with `registrations[]` populated. Verify the round trip yourself:\n   `tokenURI → card → signature → owner(agentId)`.\n6. Pin the card to IPFS; add the `ipfs://` mirror to the registry metadata.\n7. Obtain facilitator credentials (CDP API key) or deploy your own facilitator.\n   This is the only account-like thing in the system and it is *ours*, not the\n   client's.\n8. Optional: stake in the slashing contract; register with validator sets for\n   the Validation Registry; publish TEE attestation if running in an enclave.\n9. Optional, third-party listings (never load-bearing): x402 Bazaar, A2A\n   directories, ENS text record.\n\n**Ongoing, automated:**\n\n- Republish the card on price/skill change and bump `version`; the 402 challenge\n  is the authoritative price, so a stale cached card never causes a mispayment.\n- Auto-respond to low scores via the Reputation Registry.\n- Reconcile settlement txs against issued receipts daily; a gap means we served\n  data without getting paid (or vice versa).\n\n**What we explicitly do not register:** nothing on the client's side. A new\nclient agent creates no account, holds no key with us, signs no terms, and\nappears in no list of ours. Its first-ever request can be a paid one.\n\n---\n\n## 9. Security notes\n\n- **Requirement pinning.** Never trust the `X-PAYMENT` payload's own claims.\n  Validate `to == TREASURY`, `asset == USDC`, `value >= 350000`, `network ==\n  base`, `validBefore` far enough out to survive settlement, `validAfter` in the\n  past — locally, *before* calling the facilitator. A permissive server pays\n  the facilitator's rate limit for attacker-supplied garbage.\n- **Resource binding.** The `resource` field in the challenge includes the full\n  query string, so a payment authorized for one lat/lon cannot be redirected.\n- **Clock skew.** `maxTimeoutSeconds: 60` and reject authorizations expiring in\n  under ~30s; a settle that lands after `validBefore` reverts and we have already\n  done the work.\n- **Receipt scope.** `indexLimit: 1` and a 30-day `expiry` bound a receipt to one\n  rating; a bearer receipt with no limit lets a payer farm ratings from one call.\n- **Key separation.** `TREASURY` (holds money, cold) ≠ `AGENT_OPERATOR` (signs,\n  hot, in KMS) ≠ registry NFT owner (can be a multisig / timelock). Compromise of\n  the hot signer forges receipts and attestations but moves no funds.\n- **Free-endpoint abuse.** Static, cached, IP-rate-limited. All state-changing or\n  costly work is behind payment.\n\n---\n\n## 10. Open questions\n\n1. **ERC-8004 is a draft.** The `FeedbackAuth` struct, `register` signature, and\n   registry addresses in `server.ts` must be pinned to the exact deployment you\n   mint into — verify the ABI onchain before going live rather than trusting the\n   constants here.\n2. **Facilitator vs. self-relay at launch.** A hosted facilitator is faster to\n   ship; self-relaying removes a third party from the critical path and costs a\n   gas-funded wallet plus nonce management. Which do we want on day one?\n3. **Feedback relay** (§5.2) is only needed if clients genuinely hold zero ETH\n   *and* the deployed registry lacks a signed-intent entrypoint. Confirm before\n   building it.\n4. **Price in the card vs. the 402.** Both are published; the 402 wins. Worth\n   deciding whether the card should omit the price entirely to avoid a stale\n   number circulating in third-party indexes.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":482,"cache_read_input_tokens":22560,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":482},"output_tokens":4,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"70c57b0d-8dbd-49ea-ac0e-5bfe1df64e0f","uuid":"94f25f49-2e65-46e9-a9d2-2159b65a7e8c","timestamp":"2026-07-27T11:23:26.129Z","request_id":"req_011CdSUMkmzaiBhGnZeFf2Pg"}
{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01A7PUx3Dyh1ZcpEJsa8xfgS","type":"tool_result","content":"File created successfully at: /Users/liana/Documents/prog/buidl-guidl/ethskills-evals/artifacts/standards-goal-001/2026-07-27T111530Z-claude-no-skill-3/workspace/design.md (file state is current in your context — no need to Read it back)"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"70c57b0d-8dbd-49ea-ac0e-5bfe1df64e0f","uuid":"0522567a-4ee5-48e0-9c28-6b64ff0b960c","timestamp":"2026-07-27T11:23:26.174Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"create","filePath":"/Users/liana/Documents/prog/buidl-guidl/ethskills-evals/artifacts/standards-goal-001/2026-07-27T111530Z-claude-no-skill-3/workspace/design.md","content":"# forecast.example.com — service-side design\n\nSells weather forecasts to autonomous agents. $0.35 per call, settled in USDC on\nBase. No humans anywhere in the loop: no signup, no API keys, no invoices, no\ndocs to read.\n\nThe whole design is three standards stacked on one HTTP endpoint:\n\n| Constraint | Mechanism |\n|---|---|\n| Cold discovery + trust, no catalog we own | **ERC-8004** Identity / Reputation / Validation registries on Base + a machine-readable Agent Card |\n| Per-call billing, no accounts | **x402** (HTTP `402 Payment Required` + `X-PAYMENT` header) |\n| Caller has USDC but no ETH | **EIP-3009** `transferWithAuthorization`, broadcast by an x402 facilitator that pays gas |\n| Post-call rating other agents can act on | ERC-8004 **Reputation Registry**, gated by a signed receipt we hand out with the response |\n\nEverything a stranger needs is reachable from one onchain read plus one HTTPS\nGET. Nothing in the path is operated by us except the origin itself.\n\n---\n\n## 1. End-to-end flow\n\n```\n                                            ┌───────────────────────────┐\n  (0) cold discovery                        │  Base L2 (chainId 8453)   │\n  client indexes Registered events  ───────►│  ERC-8004 IdentityRegistry│\n  from the registry, gets tokenURI          │  (ERC-721, permissionless)│\n                                            └───────────┬───────────────┘\n  (1) GET tokenURI                                      │ agentId 4711\n      → /.well-known/agent-card.json                    │\n      verify registrations[].signature ◄────────────────┘  bidirectional bind\n\n  (2) read reputation before calling ──────► ERC-8004 ReputationRegistry\n      NewFeedback(agentId=4711, score, tag)  (+ ValidationRegistry, + stake)\n\n  (3) GET /v1/forecast?lat=..&lon=..\n      ◄── 402 + { accepts: [ exact / base / USDC / 350000 / payTo ] }\n\n  (4) client signs EIP-3009 authorization  (offline, no gas, no ETH)\n      GET /v1/forecast?... with X-PAYMENT: base64(payload)\n\n  (5) server → facilitator POST /verify        (signature, balance, nonce)\n      server → upstream weather source          (do the work first)\n      server → facilitator POST /settle        (facilitator pays the gas)\n                                     └──► USDC.transferWithAuthorization on Base\n\n  (6) 200 OK + forecast\n          X-PAYMENT-RESPONSE: base64({ success, transaction, network })\n          body.receipt = EIP-712 FeedbackAuth signed by the agent operator\n\n  (7) client → ReputationRegistry.giveFeedback(4711, score, tag, uri, hash, auth)\n      → onchain, readable by every future client at step (2)\n```\n\nSteps 0–7 are all machine-executable. No human reads anything.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Identity and discovery\n\n### 2.1 Root of discovery: the ERC-8004 Identity Registry\n\nWe mint an ERC-8004 agent identity on Base. The Identity Registry is an ERC-721\ncontract, permissionless to register in, deployed at the same address across\nchains, and **operated by nobody** — we do not own it, and neither does any\nother service. That is what satisfies \"not a catalog you own or operate.\"\n\n```\nIdentityRegistry.register(tokenURI, metadata[]) -> agentId (uint256, the NFT id)\n```\n\n- `agentId` = 4711 (ours, after minting).\n- Owner = our **operator wallet** (`AGENT_OPERATOR`), the address that also\n  signs receipts. The `payTo` address for USDC can be a separate treasury\n  address; the card states both.\n- `tokenURI` → `https://forecast.example.com/.well-known/agent-card.json`\n  with an IPFS mirror (`ipfs://…`) in `metadata` for censorship resistance.\n\nAny client discovers us by scanning `Registered` / `Transfer` logs from that one\ncontract over any RPC, subgraph, or third-party indexer. There is no privileged\nindex. If our website is down, the identity still exists onchain; if the\nregistry is congested, the Agent Card still resolves over HTTPS.\n\nAlternate, strictly optional discovery surfaces (third-party, never the root of\ntrust): the x402 Bazaar, A2A directory crawlers, ENS text record\n`forecast.eth → agent-card` pointing at the same JSON.\n\n### 2.2 The Agent Card\n\nServed at `https://forecast.example.com/.well-known/agent-card.json`,\n`Content-Type: application/json`, CORS `*`, cacheable. It is an A2A Agent Card\nextended with the ERC-8004 fields. Abridged:\n\n```jsonc\n{\n  \"protocolVersion\": \"0.3.0\",\n  \"name\": \"forecast.example.com\",\n  \"description\": \"Point weather forecasts, 0-168h, global. $0.35/call, USDC on Base.\",\n  \"url\": \"https://forecast.example.com/v1\",\n  \"version\": \"1.2.0\",\n  \"capabilities\": { \"streaming\": false, \"pushNotifications\": false },\n  \"defaultInputModes\": [\"application/json\"],\n  \"defaultOutputModes\": [\"application/json\"],\n\n  \"skills\": [{\n    \"id\": \"point-forecast\",\n    \"name\": \"Point forecast\",\n    \"description\": \"Hourly + daily forecast for a lat/lon, up to 168 hours ahead.\",\n    \"tags\": [\"weather\", \"forecast\", \"x402\"],\n    \"inputModes\": [\"application/json\"],\n    \"outputModes\": [\"application/json\"]\n  }],\n\n  // ERC-8004: closes the loop back to the onchain identity.\n  \"registrations\": [{\n    \"agentId\": 4711,\n    \"agentRegistry\": \"eip155:8453:0x00000000000000000000000000000000DeaDBeef\", // CAIP-10\n    \"signature\": \"0x…\"   // AGENT_OPERATOR signs (agentId, agentRegistry, cardHash)\n  }],\n\n  // ERC-8004: which trust models we support, so a client can pick a check.\n  \"trustModels\": [\"reputation\", \"crypto-economic\"],\n\n  // How to pay. Mirrors the 402 challenge; the 402 is authoritative.\n  \"x402\": {\n    \"x402Version\": 1,\n    \"endpoints\": [{\n      \"resource\": \"https://forecast.example.com/v1/forecast\",\n      \"method\": \"GET\",\n      \"scheme\": \"exact\",\n      \"network\": \"base\",\n      \"asset\": \"0x833589fCD6eDb6E08f4c7C32D4f71b54bdA02913\",\n      \"maxAmountRequired\": \"350000\",       // 0.35 USDC, 6 decimals\n      \"payTo\": \"0x…treasury\",\n      \"maxTimeoutSeconds\": 60\n    }]\n  },\n\n  \"feedback\": {\n    \"registry\": \"eip155:8453:0x…ReputationRegistry\",\n    \"authScheme\": \"erc8004-feedback-auth-v1\",\n    \"signer\": \"0x…AGENT_OPERATOR\"\n  },\n\n  \"openapi\": \"https://forecast.example.com/openapi.json\"\n}\n```\n\n**Bidirectional binding** is the point. Onchain → offchain via `tokenURI`.\nOffchain → onchain via `registrations[].signature`, produced by the key that\nowns the NFT. A client verifies both directions before trusting the card;\notherwise anyone could publish a card claiming our `agentId`, or point a\nregistry entry at somebody else's domain.\n\n### 2.3 Machine-readable interface\n\n`GET /openapi.json` — full OpenAPI 3.1 for `/v1/forecast`, including the 402\nresponse schema. An agent that can read OpenAPI needs nothing else to construct\nthe call. The Agent Card's `skills[]` covers agents that plan over A2A skills\ninstead. Both describe the same endpoint.\n\n---\n\n## 3. How a stranger judges whether to trust us\n\nA client that has never met us has four independent signals, none of which\nrequire asking us:\n\n1. **Reputation (ERC-8004 Reputation Registry).** Read `NewFeedback` events\n   filtered to `agentId = 4711`. Each carries a 0–100 score, up to two tags\n   (e.g. `accuracy`, `latency`), and an optional URI+hash to a detailed JSON\n   feedback file. Because feedback is authorized by a receipt we issue only\n   after a settled payment (§5), a rating implies a real $0.35 call —\n   sybil ratings cost money. Clients can weight by the rater's own `agentId`\n   reputation, filter to raters they know, or discount ours entirely.\n2. **Validation (ERC-8004 Validation Registry).** We accept validation requests:\n   a third-party validator re-runs a sample of our forecasts against ground\n   truth and posts `validationResponse(requestHash, score, uri, tag)` onchain.\n   This is trust that does not come from our own customers.\n3. **Crypto-economic.** The operator wallet stakes USDC in a slashing contract\n   named in the card. A client can size its exposure against the bond.\n4. **Response signing.** Every 200 carries `X-Agent-Signature`: the operator key\n   signs `keccak(agentId ‖ requestHash ‖ responseHash ‖ timestamp)`. That makes\n   any response attributable and disputable — a client can post a bad response\n   as evidence in a feedback file, and the signature proves we produced it.\n\nDegradation is graceful: a brand-new agent has no feedback, so clients fall back\nto (2)/(3)/small trial calls. $0.35 makes a trial call cheap, which is the real\nanswer to cold-start.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Payment: x402 over EIP-3009\n\n### 4.1 The 402 challenge\n\nAn unpaid request gets:\n\n```http\nHTTP/1.1 402 Payment Required\nContent-Type: application/json\n\n{\n  \"x402Version\": 1,\n  \"error\": \"X-PAYMENT header is required\",\n  \"accepts\": [{\n    \"scheme\": \"exact\",\n    \"network\": \"base\",\n    \"maxAmountRequired\": \"350000\",\n    \"resource\": \"https://forecast.example.com/v1/forecast?lat=52.52&lon=13.40\",\n    \"description\": \"Point weather forecast, 0-168h\",\n    \"mimeType\": \"application/json\",\n    \"payTo\": \"0x…treasury\",\n    \"maxTimeoutSeconds\": 60,\n    \"asset\": \"0x833589fCD6eDb6E08f4c7C32D4f71b54bdA02913\",\n    \"extra\": { \"name\": \"USD Coin\", \"version\": \"2\" }\n  }]\n}\n```\n\n`extra.name` / `extra.version` are the USDC EIP-712 domain fields the client\nneeds to build a valid EIP-3009 signature. **Read them from the token contract\nat boot** (`name()`, `EIP712_DOMAIN` / `version()`) rather than hardcoding — a\nmismatch produces signatures that fail onchain with no useful error.\n\nPrice is $0.35 flat, expressed as `350000` atomic units (USDC has 6 decimals).\nThere is no exchange-rate step: USDC is the unit of account, so \"$0.35\" and\n\"350000 units\" are the same statement and no oracle is involved.\n\n### 4.2 The payment header\n\nThe client signs an EIP-3009 `TransferWithAuthorization` and sends:\n\n```\nX-PAYMENT: base64({\n  \"x402Version\": 1,\n  \"scheme\": \"exact\",\n  \"network\": \"base\",\n  \"payload\": {\n    \"signature\": \"0x…\",                    // EIP-712 over the USDC domain\n    \"authorization\": {\n      \"from\": \"0x…client\",\n      \"to\":   \"0x…treasury\",\n      \"value\": \"350000\",\n      \"validAfter\":  \"0\",\n      \"validBefore\": \"1753600000\",\n      \"nonce\": \"0x… (32 random bytes)\"\n    }\n  }\n})\n```\n\nThis is a **signature, not a transaction**. The client never touches the\nmempool. This is exactly how the \"USDC but no ETH\" constraint is satisfied:\n`transferWithAuthorization` is executed by whoever submits it, and that submitter\npays the gas. Native USDC on Base implements EIP-3009 (`0x8335…2913`), which is\nwhy Base + native USDC is the correct pair here — bridged USDbC does not have\nthe same guarantees.\n\n### 4.3 Verification and settlement\n\nThe resource server does not broadcast anything. It calls a **facilitator**:\n\n- `POST {FACILITATOR}/verify` — `{ x402Version, paymentPayload, paymentRequirements }`\n  → `{ isValid, invalidReason, payer }`. Checks signature validity, `from`\n  balance, nonce unused, deadline not passed, and that the authorization matches\n  our requirements.\n- `POST {FACILITATOR}/settle` — same body → `{ success, transaction, network, payer }`.\n  The facilitator submits `transferWithAuthorization` on Base **and pays the ETH\n  gas**.\n\nOrdering in the handler matters and is deliberate:\n\n```\nverify  →  fetch upstream forecast  →  settle  →  200 + data\n```\n\nVerify first so we do no work for a bogus payment. Do the work before settle so\nan upstream outage returns `502` and the caller is never charged (their\nauthorization simply expires unused — nothing to refund, because we hold no\nbalances). Settle before responding so we never hand over data for a payment\nthat fails to land. If settle fails after the work is done, we eat the upstream\ncost and return `402` with the facilitator's reason.\n\nWe can drop the facilitator dependency at any time by broadcasting\n`transferWithAuthorization` ourselves from a gas-funded relayer wallet; the\nverify/settle seam in `server.ts` is a single interface (`Facilitator`) with two\nmethods, precisely so that swap is local. Facilitator choice is an operational\ndependency, not a protocol one.\n\n### 4.4 Replay, idempotency, refunds\n\n- **Double-spend** is prevented onchain: the EIP-3009 nonce is consumed by USDC\n  itself, so a replayed authorization reverts.\n- **Retries** are handled offchain: we key a short-lived cache on the\n  authorization nonce. A repeat of an already-settled request returns the cached\n  forecast plus the original tx hash instead of a rejection. Without this, a\n  client whose connection drops after settle has paid and got nothing.\n- **No refunds.** Per-call, pay-on-success. We never hold caller funds, so there\n  is no balance to return and no invoice to reconcile. This is what the \"no\n  pre-funded balances\" constraint buys us.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Ratings\n\nThe problem: onchain feedback is only useful to future clients if it is\nexpensive to forge. The solution is to make a rating require proof of a real\npaid call, without us being able to censor negative ones.\n\n### 5.1 Receipt issued with the response\n\nEvery settled `200` includes a `receipt` object: an EIP-712 **FeedbackAuth**\nsigned by `AGENT_OPERATOR`, which is the key the Reputation Registry checks.\n\n```jsonc\n\"receipt\": {\n  \"domain\": { \"name\": \"ERC8004ReputationRegistry\", \"version\": \"1\",\n              \"chainId\": 8453, \"verifyingContract\": \"0x…ReputationRegistry\" },\n  \"primaryType\": \"FeedbackAuth\",\n  \"message\": {\n    \"agentId\": \"4711\",\n    \"clientAddress\": \"0x…payer\",        // the EIP-3009 `from`, i.e. who actually paid\n    \"indexLimit\": \"1\",                  // one rating per paid call\n    \"expiry\": \"1756200000\",             // ~30 days\n    \"chainId\": \"8453\",\n    \"identityRegistry\": \"0x…IdentityRegistry\",\n    \"signerAddress\": \"0x…AGENT_OPERATOR\"\n  },\n  \"signature\": \"0x…\",\n  \"settlementTx\": \"0x…\"                 // convenience: the USDC transfer\n}\n```\n\nThe client then calls, unprompted and unassisted:\n\n```\nReputationRegistry.giveFeedback(\n  agentId       = 4711,\n  score         = 0..100,\n  tag1          = \"accuracy\",\n  tag2          = \"latency\",\n  fileuri       = \"ipfs://…\"   // optional detailed review\n  filehash      = 0x…,\n  feedbackAuth  = <abi-encoded receipt above>\n)\n```\n\nEmitted as `NewFeedback(agentId, clientAddress, score, tag1, tag2, …)` — which\nis exactly what step (2) of the flow reads. The loop closes.\n\nProperties that matter:\n\n- **We cannot suppress a bad rating.** The receipt is issued *before* we know the\n  score, is bearer-usable for 30 days, and lands on a contract we do not control.\n  Refusing to issue receipts at all is itself visible: the ratio of settlement\n  txs to feedback events is public.\n- **We cannot forge ratings for ourselves.** The auth binds `clientAddress` to\n  the payer, so a fake rating costs a real $0.35 from a distinct funded address.\n- **Revisions and replies.** ERC-8004 supports the client revoking its feedback\n  and the agent appending a response to a specific feedback entry. We run an\n  automated responder for scores below a threshold, attaching the signed\n  response hash from §3.4 as evidence. No human writes it.\n\n### 5.2 Rating without going onchain\n\nGas is on the client for `giveFeedback`, and the constraint says clients may\nhold no ETH. Two outs, both automatic:\n\n- `POST /v1/feedback/relay` accepts an EIP-712-signed feedback intent and we\n  submit it for the client, paying gas. We cannot alter the score — it is inside\n  the client's signature — and we relay negative ones or the tx/feedback ratio\n  exposes us. (Requires the registry to support a signed-intent entrypoint; if\n  the deployed registry does not, use a generic ERC-2771 forwarder / ERC-4337\n  paymaster instead.)\n- Or the client uses any public ERC-4337 paymaster that accepts USDC for gas.\n  Nothing about this is our dependency.\n\n---\n\n## 6. HTTP surface\n\n| Method | Path | Paid | Purpose |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| GET | `/.well-known/agent-card.json` | no | ERC-8004 / A2A discovery document |\n| GET | `/.well-known/x402` | no | x402 resource list (price, asset, network) |\n| GET | `/openapi.json` | no | OpenAPI 3.1 for the paid endpoint |\n| GET | `/v1/forecast?lat&lon&hours&units` | **yes** | 402 → pay → forecast |\n| GET | `/v1/quote?lat&lon` | no | the 402 challenge alone, for planning |\n| POST | `/v1/feedback/relay` | no | gasless rating relay for ETH-less clients |\n| GET | `/v1/receipt/:nonce` | no | re-fetch a receipt for a settled call |\n| GET | `/healthz` | no | liveness + upstream + facilitator status |\n\nFree endpoints are rate-limited by IP and are cheap/static, so they are not a\nDoS lever. The paid endpoint needs no rate limit — payment *is* the rate limit.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Dependencies\n\n### 7.1 Onchain (Base mainnet, chainId 8453)\n\n| Dependency | Address / standard | Why | If it fails |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| **USDC (native)** | `0x833589fCD6eDb6E08f4c7C32D4f71b54bdA02913` | Unit of account + EIP-3009 gasless transfer | Hard dependency. No payment. |\n| **EIP-3009** | on USDC | `transferWithAuthorization` — client pays with a signature, not gas | Hard. Without it the caller needs ETH. |\n| **EIP-712** | — | Typed-data signing for both the payment and the receipt | Hard. |\n| **ERC-8004 IdentityRegistry** | ERC-721, Base, address pinned in `.env` | Cold discovery + the agentId everything hangs off | Degraded: already-registered clients still pay; new ones can't discover onchain. |\n| **ERC-8004 ReputationRegistry** | Base, address pinned | Ratings other agents read pre-purchase | Degraded: service still sells, trust signal goes stale. |\n| **ERC-8004 ValidationRegistry** | Base, address pinned | Third-party quality attestation | Optional. |\n| **Base L2** | chainId 8453 | Settlement. Sub-cent gas is what makes a $0.35 call viable | Hard. Sequencer outage stalls settle; queue and retry, serve nothing meanwhile. |\n| Staking/slashing contract | optional | Crypto-economic trust signal | Optional. |\n| ERC-2771 forwarder / 4337 paymaster | optional | Gasless `giveFeedback` for ETH-less clients | Optional; §5.2 fallback. |\n\nAddresses are **not** hardcoded in application logic — they are config, verified\nat boot (code exists at the address, `chainId` matches, USDC `name`/`version`/\n`decimals` read live). ERC-8004 is a draft; pin the exact deployment and ABI you\nmint into, and re-verify on any registry redeploy.\n\n### 7.2 Offchain\n\n| Dependency | Role | Failure mode |\n|---|---|---|\n| **x402 facilitator** (CDP or self-hosted) | `/verify` + `/settle`, pays gas on Base | Hard-ish. Fall back to a second facilitator, then to self-relaying from a gas-funded wallet. Health-checked at `/healthz`. |\n| **Base RPC** (2 providers) | Boot-time checks, event reads, receipt/tx lookups | Degraded; failover. |\n| **Weather upstream** (e.g. Open-Meteo / NOAA) | The actual product | Hard for the paid call. Return `502` **before** settle so nobody is charged. |\n| **DNS + TLS for forecast.example.com** | Resolves `tokenURI`; TLS is what binds the card to the domain | Hard. Card unreachable = no discovery. IPFS mirror is the hedge. |\n| **IPFS pin** (Pinata/web3.storage) | Immutable mirror of the agent card + feedback files | Optional but recommended. |\n| **Key management** (KMS/HSM) for `AGENT_OPERATOR` | Signs receipts, response attestations, card binding | Hard. Compromise = forged receipts; rotate via the registry, which is why the signer is named in the card and not assumed. |\n| **Indexer** (optional, ours or a subgraph) | Read our own reputation for the auto-responder | Optional. |\n| Log/metric sink | Reconcile settlements vs. responses | Operational. |\n\nNote the asymmetry: the *client's* path depends only on Base, USDC, the ERC-8004\nregistries, and our origin. The facilitator, IPFS pin, and indexer are **our**\nconveniences and are all replaceable without the client noticing.\n\n---\n\n## 8. Registration and publication checklist\n\nOrdered. Everything before the service is findable.\n\n**Once, at setup:**\n\n1. Register `forecast.example.com`; TLS cert; serve `/.well-known/` over HTTPS\n   with CORS `*`.\n2. Generate `AGENT_OPERATOR` (signing) and `TREASURY` (payout) keys in KMS.\n   Fund `AGENT_OPERATOR` with a small amount of ETH on Base — needed **only** for\n   the one-time identity mint and any registry writes, never for serving calls.\n3. Publish `/.well-known/agent-card.json`, `/.well-known/x402`, `/openapi.json`.\n   (Chicken-and-egg: publish the card first without `registrations`, mint, then\n   republish with it. The registry `tokenURI` must resolve at mint time.)\n4. **Mint the ERC-8004 identity** on Base:\n   `IdentityRegistry.register(\"https://forecast.example.com/.well-known/agent-card.json\")`\n   → record `agentId` (4711).\n5. Sign the registration binding with `AGENT_OPERATOR` and republish the card\n   with `registrations[]` populated. Verify the round trip yourself:\n   `tokenURI → card → signature → owner(agentId)`.\n6. Pin the card to IPFS; add the `ipfs://` mirror to the registry metadata.\n7. Obtain facilitator credentials (CDP API key) or deploy your own facilitator.\n   This is the only account-like thing in the system and it is *ours*, not the\n   client's.\n8. Optional: stake in the slashing contract; register with validator sets for\n   the Validation Registry; publish TEE attestation if running in an enclave.\n9. Optional, third-party listings (never load-bearing): x402 Bazaar, A2A\n   directories, ENS text record.\n\n**Ongoing, automated:**\n\n- Republish the card on price/skill change and bump `version`; the 402 challenge\n  is the authoritative price, so a stale cached card never causes a mispayment.\n- Auto-respond to low scores via the Reputation Registry.\n- Reconcile settlement txs against issued receipts daily; a gap means we served\n  data without getting paid (or vice versa).\n\n**What we explicitly do not register:** nothing on the client's side. A new\nclient agent creates no account, holds no key with us, signs no terms, and\nappears in no list of ours. Its first-ever request can be a paid one.\n\n---\n\n## 9. Security notes\n\n- **Requirement pinning.** Never trust the `X-PAYMENT` payload's own claims.\n  Validate `to == TREASURY`, `asset == USDC`, `value >= 350000`, `network ==\n  base`, `validBefore` far enough out to survive settlement, `validAfter` in the\n  past — locally, *before* calling the facilitator. A permissive server pays\n  the facilitator's rate limit for attacker-supplied garbage.\n- **Resource binding.** The `resource` field in the challenge includes the full\n  query string, so a payment authorized for one lat/lon cannot be redirected.\n- **Clock skew.** `maxTimeoutSeconds: 60` and reject authorizations expiring in\n  under ~30s; a settle that lands after `validBefore` reverts and we have already\n  done the work.\n- **Receipt scope.** `indexLimit: 1` and a 30-day `expiry` bound a receipt to one\n  rating; a bearer receipt with no limit lets a payer farm ratings from one call.\n- **Key separation.** `TREASURY` (holds money, cold) ≠ `AGENT_OPERATOR` (signs,\n  hot, in KMS) ≠ registry NFT owner (can be a multisig / timelock). Compromise of\n  the hot signer forges receipts and attestations but moves no funds.\n- **Free-endpoint abuse.** Static, cached, IP-rate-limited. All state-changing or\n  costly work is behind payment.\n\n---\n\n## 10. Open questions\n\n1. **ERC-8004 is a draft.** The `FeedbackAuth` struct, `register` signature, and\n   registry addresses in `server.ts` must be pinned to the exact deployment you\n   mint into — verify the ABI onchain before going live rather than trusting the\n   constants here.\n2. **Facilitator vs. self-relay at launch.** A hosted facilitator is faster to\n   ship; self-relaying removes a third party from the critical path and costs a\n   gas-funded wallet plus nonce management. Which do we want on day one?\n3. **Feedback relay** (§5.2) is only needed if clients genuinely hold zero ETH\n   *and* the deployed registry lacks a signed-intent entrypoint. Confirm before\n   building it.\n4. **Price in the card vs. the 402.** Both are published; the 402 wins. Worth\n   deciding whether the card should omit the price entirely to avoid a stale\n   number circulating in third-party indexes.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null,"userModified":false}}
